* Status, decision, and provenance

This note is the next, *non-normative* implementation artifact after the v893
formal-proof handover.  Install it as =doc/claim-proof-obligation-ledger.org=.
It is NOT a replacement for any part of the canonical 12-part R4 design and
NOT a proof certificate.  It contains no new Gesso runtime APIs.  The user
continues to author ordinary Clojure and Gesso constructions, never Quint,
Recife, TLA+, or a Gesso-specific proof DSL as an application requirement.

Inspected source: the 2026-10-14 Gesso archive, branch
=application-assurance=, HEAD =0415a69= (v892 installed).  Its full test gates
were reported passing by the developer.  This note neither reruns those gates
nor treats them as a mathematical proof.  HumanHelp source is *not* in the
supplied archive; paths or bindings from R4's illustrative Claim example are
requirements/candidates, NOT independently checked production facts.

Normative anchors:
- R4 Part IV: authoritative operations, trusted command identity, commit
  guards, duplicate/retry outcomes and progression.
- R4 Part VIII: Claim, especially the production lifecycle path, the worked
  Claim example and browser/advisory behavior.
- R4 Part IX: proof obligations, dependencies, reusable evidence and honest
  assumptions.
- R4 Part X: "Worked correspondence slice: HumanHelp Claim has one production
  meaning" and "Formal obligations must return to executable production
  boundaries".
- R4 Part XII: Gates B, D, E, K, M, N and O, plus the staged proof pilot.
- v893 handover: the proof-first priority, before further diagnostic wrappers.
- Exploratory v781: generic resolution templates are a possible reuse
  mechanism, NOT a required new abstraction or an established theorem.

** Decision for the first mechanically checked subject

Pilot exactly ONE Request and TWO competing helpers.  Check the *server-side
atomic Claim incorporation and stable command-result binding* first.  Include
membership revocation as an interfering transition and duplicate/retry of an
existing command.  Deliberately exclude Unclaim, Cancel, Reassign, transport,
SSE, DOM and liveness from the FIRST theorem; those need separate proofs and
refinement maps, not silent assumptions about this theorem.

The important theorem is NOT merely "the owner field is scalar."  The pilot
must establish that *at most one distinct initial-epoch Claim command can be
incorporated*, even with two helpers, stale preparation, retries, and a
membership-revocation race; an incorporated command's binding and recorded
outcome remain stable.  A model admitting two successful Claim incorporations
before an Unclaim/epoch change must produce a counterexample.

All Claims below refer to a single Request in an initial, still-open ownership
epoch.  A later lifecycle extension must introduce an explicit ownership epoch
or release/reopen semantics; a Request can legitimately be claimed again in a
new epoch.  No current Gesso API is claimed to generate the pilot model.

* Precise abstract machine to formalize

** State and constants

| Symbol | Kind | Semantics / origin |
|--------+------+--------------------|
| =RequestId= | constant | One Request identity; R4 Part VIII |
| =Helpers= | finite set | Exactly two distinct helpers in the first TLC instance |
| =CommandIds= | finite set | At least two command IDs and a compatible retry of one |
| =OrganizationId= | constant | Request's Organization; no independently chosen browser authority |
| =owner= | =None= or helper | Current authoritative Request owner |
| =requestStatus= | =open= or =claimed= | Persisted Request state in this first epoch |
| =memberStatus[h]= | =active= or =inactive= | Current authoritative Membership status |
| =memberOrg[h]= | Organization ID | Membership-to-Request association |
| =organizationPolicy= | finite policy value | Mutable policy determining Claim eligibility |
| =ledger= | partial function command ID -> record | Incorporated authoritative command-result records |
| =acceptedEvents= | sequence of ghost events | MODEL-ONLY audit history of accepted Claims; NOT claimed production storage |
| =attempts= | sequence or finite queue | MODEL-ONLY submitted/retried command bindings; never authoritative identity |

Each semantic command *binding* is a tuple of =command-id=,
=authenticated-principal=, =:request/claim=, and arguments containing the
Request ID.  The browser may carry its command ID and arguments, but the
principal is obtained from a trusted server context; never trust a principal
sent as browser bytes.  In the model, an attempted binding is constructed at
the trusted boundary.  If comparing to physical requests, model command-ID
validation and trusted binding are explicit separate obligations.

An accepted event has =command-id=, =principal=, =request-id=, and the
pre-commit eligibility decision.  It is a ghost variable for specifying a
history property.  It does NOT assert that existing =gesso_commands= records
persist the entire eligibility snapshot.  In particular, an accepted Claim
remains valid history if the helper's Membership is revoked AFTER the commit.

First-model assumptions to state explicitly, not infer:
- Initially Request is open/unclaimed, ledger and acceptedEvents are empty.
- Exactly one Request and one ownership epoch are modeled.
- Each server-side resolution sees a coherent *prestate* for all admission
  dependencies, and commit of the guarded Request change PLUS one durable
  command-result record is atomic/serializable.
- Only admitted resolution transitions can write the ledger or Request owner.
  The real application's other writers are NOT thereby proved absent.
- Membership revocation and Organization policy changes are serialized with
  resolution at the modeled authority boundary, not arbitrarily interleaved
  halfway through its guards/writes.
- The =AllowsClaim= predicate is a *named, application-owned* policy premise.
  The proof checks its use, not whether that policy is socially desirable or
  whether the production Clojure implementation faithfully computes it.
- A compatible retry can observe an incorporated record; a missing record is
  =unknown/unresolved= rather than proof of rejection or permission to resend
  unchanged assumptions indefinitely.

** Actions (candidate formal vocabulary)

=ClaimNew(c, h)= is admitted only when there is NO incorporated record for =c=
and the trusted binding is for =h=, =:request/claim= and this Request.
Eligibility must be checked against the state participating in the SAME
atomic commit:

#+begin_example
requestStatus = open
owner = None
memberStatus[h] = active
memberOrg[h] = OrganizationId
AllowsClaim(organizationPolicy, h, RequestId)
no conflicting command ledger entry for c
#+end_example

If satisfied, ONE abstract atomic transition changes:

#+begin_example
requestStatus' = claimed
owner' = h
ledger'[c] = {binding: (c,h,:request/claim,RequestId),
              disposition: accepted, outcome: accepted-claim}
acceptedEvents' = Append(acceptedEvents, matching ghost event)
#+end_example

The exact outcome shape is an application-model question; the above is not an
assertion that production records already have that schema.  A failed guard
must NOT write ownership or a success record.  Model a rejected *attempt* as
state-preserving for Request/ledger, with a non-authorizing rejection result;
the first proof does not require durable recording of every rejection.  A
separately specified policy is needed for duplicate or rejected-command IDs
that are not yet incorporated.

=Replay(c, presentedBinding)=, if an authoritative record exists, leaves
Request and ledger unchanged.  It exposes the recorded outcome ONLY after
trusted disclosure authorization and an exact binding match.  Incompatible
reuse and unauthorized disclosure never execute a fresh domain mutation.
Withheld results must not leak a record's private binding or contents.

=RevokeMembership(h)= changes membership state, not the existing ownership
history.  If it occurs BEFORE =ClaimNew=, the Claim cannot incorporate; if it
occurs AFTER =ClaimNew=, the accepted event still describes eligibility at
incorporation.  =ChangeOrganizationPolicy= similarly tests the policy race.

=EnqueueAttempt=, =LoseReply=, =DuplicateAttempt= and =DeliverAttempt= are
OPTIONAL model-environment actions for finite concurrency exploration; they
must not be confused with server authority.  The initial safety theorem may
use a finite nondeterministic choice of Claim/replay/revoke/policy actions
without modeling a full network queue.

** Proof obligations, not merely executable examples

| ID | Obligation | Intended proof boundary | Status at v894 |
|----+------------+-------------------------+----------------|
| CLM-S1 | Every accepted event arose at a commit where trusted principal, open Request, active associated Membership and policy admitted Claim | One abstract atomic authority step; application policy assumed truthful | not proved |
| CLM-S2 | In the initial epoch no two *distinct* Claim command IDs have accepted events for this Request | Domain safety, concurrent helpers / stale preparation | not proved |
| CLM-S3 | One incorporated command ID never changes binding or recorded outcome | Generic durable resolution template | not proved |
| CLM-S4 | A matching replay does not cause a second domain effect; mismatched reuse never admits a fresh claim | Durable lookup and resolver action | not proved |
| CLM-S5 | Rejection, disclosure denial and unresolved delivery cannot create owner/authority facts | Authority / knowledge separation at server boundary | not proved |
| CLM-R1 | Every Gesso abstract transaction step maps to one ClaimDomain action or allowed stuttering, *provided its modeled premises match* | Reference =tx-model= versus chosen Claim spec | not established |
| CLM-R2 | Every actual admitted XTDB Claim commit/rejection refines one abstract Claim step or stutters | Physical transaction isolation + Gesso model lowering + HumanHelp callback | not established |
| CLM-B1 | Provisional Claim UI cannot become authoritative without appropriate authoritative evidence | Browser adapter/participant projection | deferred, not established |
| CLM-G1 | Composed server/browser/network observable traces refine the admitted global choreography | Whole-system refinement | deferred, not established |
| CLM-L1 | Claim eventually settles or recovers under explicit delivery/fairness/availability assumptions | Temporal liveness | deferred, not established |

A *successful finite TLC check* for two helpers and finite commands would
establish that no reachable counterexample exists in the admitted finite
instance, subject to its exact modeled assumptions.  A *checked inductive
invariant theorem* would support a more general claim over the defined
transition relation, but STILL NOT establish the Clojure, XTDB, or browser
refinement obligations.  Keep the evidence classes separate.

** Concrete adversarial mutations expected to fail

- Mutant M1: remove the Request-open/current-owner guard from incorporation.
  The second helper can commit after the first; CLM-S2 fails even though
  =owner= still stores a single helper at a time.
- Mutant M2: allow an existing ledger entry to be replaced when the same
  command ID arrives with different arguments; CLM-S3 fails.
- Mutant M3: check Membership eligibility before a revocation, then write
  without validating the Membership/Organization dependencies at the commit;
  CLM-S1 fails under a revocation/claim interleaving in a *concrete two-phase*
  model.  A purely atomic specification cannot catch a concrete TOCTOU flaw
  by itself; CLM-R2 must expose it.
- Mutant M4: treat a missing command record following network uncertainty as
  definitive business rejection; violates future participant-knowledge
  obligations, not necessarily CLM-S1/S2.  Do not misclassify it as a server
  ownership-safety counterexample.

A useful first formal model MUST fail M1 and M2.  M3 belongs in the concrete
server/refinement companion; M4 belongs in the later browser/network model.

** Production-assumption ledger

| Assumption | Production obligation | Current disposition |
|------------+-----------------------+---------------------|
| ATOMIC-COMMIT | Command-result insert, guarded Request ownership write and all declared predicates are one atomic/serializable XTDB transaction | Open; Gesso plan construction exists, no inspected HumanHelp binding |
| COMMAND-ID | Browser correlation ID bound to trusted principal, operation and arguments, and checked on retry | Partially supported by trusted protocol and =command_resolution=; application storage/replay path open |
| PERMISSION | Principal authentication and disclosure of prior result are server-owned | Trusted boundary exists; application permission predicate open |
| COMPLETE-GUARDS | Request, Membership, Organization, association and any other policy dependencies guarded at incorporation | Open; no complete production policy inventory |
| COHERENT-PRESTATE | Gesso's observed dependencies and modeled reference correspond to one suitable authority point | Not established by v891/v892 predecessor comparisons |
| FULL-WRITER-SET | No alternate production writer can violate Request/ledger invariants | Open; whole-application write inventory needed |
| RECORD-IMMUTABILITY | Once incorporated, no path overwrites command binding/outcome | =incorporation-plan= prevents duplicate create; global table writer closure not proved |
| CALLBACK-FAITHFULNESS | Model =enabled?= / =prepare= / policy and actual trusted execute callback have the same critical semantics | Open; independently authored callbacks can agree on samples but diverge elsewhere |
| FINITE-REPRESENTATIVE | Two-helper finite input reflects claimed bounded theorem scope | Only a bounded-model premise, never production completeness |
| BROWSER-EFFECTS | Shell realizes normalized browser adapter events/effects correctly | Deferred; tests and real Chromium are needed but do not prove all traces |
| NETWORK-PROGRESS | Delivery/recovery assumptions adequate for stated conditional liveness | Deferred; no unconditional eventual success claim |

No hash, certificate-looking map, correlated outcome, or caller-selected
modeled predecessor upgrades an open assumption to discharged status.

* Refinement direction and the v891/v892 evidence

Formal safety is a property of the abstract transition relation.  For a
TRANSFERRED implementation claim, define an abstraction function =alpha=
from concrete durable Request/Membership/Organization/command records (plus
trusted protocol context) to the formal state.  A suitable simulation
obligation is:

#+begin_example
InitConcrete(c0) => InitAbstract(alpha(c0))

ConcreteStep(c, c') =>
  AbstractNext(alpha(c), alpha(c'))
  OR alpha(c) = alpha(c')    ; explicit allowed stuttering
#+end_example

The relation must quantify over admitted server effects, conflicting writers,
retries, result lookup/disclosure and any execution path that can change the
abstract variables.  An observed matching plan alone is insufficient.

The existing v891/v892 controlled comparator contributes ONE useful premise:
it can compare an executor-bound captured prestate with the private state at
one chosen finite modeled occurrence, and compare the relevant modeled edge
and submitted plan under its declared scope.  It does not prove that the
physical execution started there, that every read was captured, that no write
intervened, or that the formal model's transitions are complete.  Thus it is
*local correspondence-test evidence*, not a proof of the simulation formula.

The first formal theorem should consume the *same semantic subject* as the
chosen production Claim operation, with explicit provenance and a declared
lowering/refinement boundary.  A separately hand-authored TLA Claim model
that happens to resemble an independently authored Clojure callback is useful
for discovery, but cannot satisfy Gate B without correspondence evidence.

* Formal backend and next two concrete artifacts

No external formal checker was run for v894.  This artifact specifies the
obligations and identifies their production consumers.  The initial proof
engineering can use hand-authored TLA+ while the automatic elaboration path
is under development; it must remain clearly classified as *prototype*.

** v895 — first executable formal subject

Create exactly one finite TLC-checkable =ClaimDomainSpec.tla= (or a
justified equivalent) with =Init=, =Next=, the Claim, Replay, Revocation and
PolicyChange actions, and named CLM-S1 through CLM-S4.  Fix the state universe
to one Request, two helpers and at least two command IDs.  Include bad mutants
M1/M2 as checkable negative configurations, not edits to the canonical
semantics.  Record exact tool version, config, bound, exploration result and
counterexample for each mutant.  No checked result before a real checker run.

If a formal toolchain is unavailable, keep the model artifact and mark checker
execution blocked; do not fabricate a successful theorem or substitute unit
tests.  The model is a research prototype, not a new application authoring
interface or independent source of HumanHelp policy truth.

** v896 or later — concrete correspondence slice

Before claiming a physical refinement, obtain current HumanHelp production
Request/Membership/Organization source or a verified operation binding and
inspect the *actual* Claim command, guards, typed settlement and durable
record path.  Either develop a genuinely checked bridge for the supported
transaction subset, use runtime conformance with its weaker evidence class,
or retain the implementation boundary as an explicit premise.

The next browser obligation should abstract =gesso.live.browser.adapter/step=
with separate provisional, advisory and authoritative knowledge.  It must not
reinterpret timeout/unknown delivery as rejected business truth, and must
keep the formal/prod bridge honest about HTMX and DOM shell behavior.

This is planning/provenance evidence only.  It does not discharge CLM-S1..S5,
CLM-R1/R2, CLM-B1, CLM-G1 or CLM-L1.

** Original obligation reconciliation (not theorem discharge)

| Obligation | New evidence for this fixture | Still required |

The v894 abstract failed-attempt step leaves the ledger unchanged. The v2481
physical loser instead adds a durable rejection record guarded by the claimed
Request. If =alpha= includes the full ledger, this is NOT stuttering. Either
extend the abstract machine with a guarded journal-only rejection step (and
prove preservation and immutable replay for it), or explicitly project only
accepted records for the narrower initial-epoch ownership theorem. The latter
projection cannot prove rejection replay or disclosure properties. Do not
silently identify all incorporated records with accepted Claims: this trace
has two command records but only one accepted business effect.

** Existing report boundary and next enforcement step

=src/gesso/assurance/report.clj= requires an issued checked-plan operation,
original proof/obligation source and optional rechecked application binding.
Its =:classification :tested= is caller-reported-only, unauthenticated;
=:full-success?= remains false. =inductive_report.clj= similarly does not
establish physical operation coverage. This fixture invokes
=publication/build-executor=, not those issued proof-bound handles. Do not
attach its observations to a different Claim proof simply because the name
matches; do not manufacture a certificate or change report verdicts.

Next: bind the fixture's declared operation to an original independent
semantic subject and the existing source-bound checker. Start with the
success and guarded refusal alternatives and distinguish acceptance history
from journal incorporation. Derive/check the supported transaction mapping
and add adversarial same-subject controls before claiming a report-level
correspondence. Keep production writer inventory, HumanHelp, browser gate,
formal soundness and liveness separate. No original CLM obligation or full
work package is retired by this documentation checkpoint.

guarded refusal, accepted replay and rejected replay). The abstract refusal
writes its own journal slot; replay frames all declared state. The v2495

is neither independent rule soundness nor a certificate for the installed
application.

* v2550 — selected mapped prestate guard refinement

The v2548–v2549 prefix is derived from the complete supplied mapped rows,
including framed/read-only documents and both command journal slots. The
same preparation supplies the projected abstract prestate and the exact
pre-write assertions. An admitted transaction preserves its original
canonical suffix. These checks do not independently prove SQL semantics.

For a checked mapping M, let R be the prepared complete selected rows,
P_M(R) their abstract projection, G_M(R) the generated guard prefix, and
D the actual transaction-internal pre-write state. The required safety
implication is:

#+begin_example
  execute_assertions(G_M(R), D) = pass
    => P_M(selected_rows(D)) = P_M(R)
#+end_example

This implication must hold under the admitted XTDB value/column codec,
slot selection and row acquisition semantics at the transaction's actual
read basis. Its converse is not required for safety: exact row guards
may reject changes outside the abstract projection. Sound refusal and
progress for unsupported values require separate treatment.

The physical refusal-prefix regression deliberately changes the request,
assignment, each membership, the occupied journal outcome or cardinality,
and the previously empty slot. It requires atomic refusal without a
new loser record, and acceptance after restoring the exact prepared rows.
This is finite correspondence evidence for the selected closed fixture,
not a proof of the implication above. CLM-R2 remains open; in particular
SQL/codec refinement, transaction-internal read basis, writer mediation,
historical retention, and production HumanHelp correspondence remain
unestablished. Neither historical verification nor a matching prefix
alone discharges these premises.

* v2561 — decoded guard correspondence and abstract projection

The v2551–v2560 opt-in work separates independent closed decoded-row
predicates, source-relative exact SQL prefix checks, structural rules,
concrete entry encodings and kernel-checked encoded cardinality and
congruence lemmas. The decoded audit rechecks the source-relative
structure, entry round trips and exact proof goals. It does not establish
universal encoding or SQL semantics.

The v2561 pilot additionally projects both the prepared rows and the
reconstructed observed rows through the actual typed mapped projection
using the same supplied outcome decoder. It requires equality of the two
observed abstract states. The regression includes occupied nullable
coordinates and both journal slots, a decoder sensitive to nested command
arguments, and a stateful decoder whose differing outputs must be rejected.

This is finite decoded-instance correspondence. The proof kernel checks
encoded list equalities, while Clojure checks decoded entry correspondence
and projection equality. No CLM-R2 premise is retired: general decoded
encoding, recursive composition and projection refinement, decoder
congruence, faithful acquisition, shared SQL formatting and identifier
normalization, SQL value and struct semantics and the transaction-internal
read basis remain open. Physical writer mediation and historical retention
remain separate obligations. No certification or production authority is
conferred by the pilot. Further provider work must address the implication
in v2550, not merely add equalities over already equal decoded values.

* v2562 — valid-time distinction at the physical assertion boundary

The mapped projection regression uses one pinned system snapshot and two
fixed valid-time clocks. A required document changes, a nullable document
appears, an occupied journal slot changes outcome, and a previously empty
slot becomes occupied. The independent decoded predicate distinguishes
all four differences. Generated prefixes with one stale coordinate at a
time, or the complete stale observation, must refuse without writing a
marker. The current observation must permit the marker to commit. This is
finite physical evidence, not direct inspection of an internal read basis
or a general theorem of XTDB temporal semantics.

The initial positive control exposed a separate conservative refusal:
an occupied journal slot with empty arguments can fail struct equality
when another record has nonempty nested arguments. Individual physical
assertions isolated the failure to the occupied-slot equality guard.
The temporal regression uses uniform nonempty argument shapes to isolate
its intended property. Empty-map equality and progress under mixed struct
schemas remain open; do not replace equality with LENGTH alone, which
would fail to distinguish empty maps from other empty value shapes. No
production guard semantics or certification claims change in this revision.

* v2563 — empty decoded-map guard under the installed XTDB codec

XTDB 2.2.0-beta1 exposes no SQL TYPEOF or IS OF predicate in the tested
fragment. Its LENGTH implementation counts non-null struct members but
also accepts strings, binary values, lists and sets. Its STR implementation
prints decoded structs with a leading brace, including schema fields with
null values. For the other LENGTH-supported types, a zero-length value
cannot have that prefix: strings are empty, binary prints as hexadecimal,
and list/set values print as vectors. The restricted empty-map lowering
therefore combines parameterized LENGTH = 0 and STR LIKE '{%' conditions.
The independent prefix checker reconstructs both conditions (version 2).

The physical regression restores mixed empty/nonempty argument shapes in
the v2562 temporal fixture and exercises both empty arguments and a nested
empty map. Nonempty maps, empty strings, map-looking strings, empty lists,
sets, binary values, null and absent fields must refuse without a marker
write. Restored decoded rows must commit despite intervening schema changes.
A null-only struct decodes to an empty map in the installed provider; the
obligation concerns decoded visible entries, not preservation of explicit
physical null field identities.

This is a version-specific codec qualification, not a portable SQL type
predicate or a general refinement proof. A provider upgrade must requalify
both LENGTH and STR semantics, including the exhaustive supported-type
boundary. No CLM-R2 premise or certification authority is retired here.

* v2564 — scalar and vector coercion at the mapped guard boundary

The embedded XTDB probe shows that ordinary SQL equality and IS NOT
DISTINCT FROM compare an integer equal to a numerically equal float or
decimal, and a keyword equal to the corresponding unprefixed string.
List equality inherits these element coercions. These are false matches
relative to the independent decoded Clojure predicates, not merely the
conservative refusals of the empty-struct problem. The internal strict
equality operation is not exposed as a SQL function in this version.

For the restricted mapped scalar fragment, conjoin ordinary equality
with equality of STR(value) and STR(ARRAY[value]) against the same bound
expected value. Scalar printing distinguishes the tested keyword/string
and integer/float pairs; singleton-array printing preserves the decimal
suffix that scalar printing omits. For supported vectors retain whole
list equality and compare every element with the scalar conjunction,
using one-based array indexing. Whole-list printing alone loses the
keyword/string distinction. Apply the scalar conjunction to mapped
required and present nullable document identities and fields and to
journal record leaves. Ordinary model version guards remain unchanged.
The independent mapped prefix checker reconstructs the journal constraints
and uses the shared low-level document formatter (version 3).

The physical regression mutates document and journal scalar fields and
vector elements, checks independent decoded mismatch and atomic refusal
without a marker, and requires a successful control after each restore.
This addresses the reproduced coercion counterexamples, not every possible
provider type, schema or read basis. The print forms and value comparison
semantics are specific to XTDB 2.2.0-beta1; provider upgrades require
requalification. No general codec theorem or CLM-R2 discharge follows.
The initial regression also exposed the existing raw nested SQL identifier
limitation for a field named keyword (a reserved token); the fixture uses
non-reserved field names. Quoting or rejecting all reserved identifier
spellings remains a separate lowering/availability issue.

* v2565 — canonical decoded field identities and nested SQL quoting

The installed XTDB normal-form conversion collapses distinct Clojure
field spellings: for example :foo_bar and :foo-bar address one column,
and :foo$bar, :foo.bar and :foo/bar address one namespaced column. Even
an isolated alias can change the decoded key relative to the supplied
prestate. Require each guarded document and journal map key to round-trip
through the default KEBAB_CASE_KEYWORD result-key decoder. Retain existing
collision detection and restricted normalized identifier syntax. This
checks supplied decoded keys, not arbitrary writes or custom key functions.

Quote each normalized journal field segment separately, including root
columns, using double quotes after the identifier syntax check. Physical
probes confirm quoted reserved tokens and namespaced fields at nested
paths. The independent checker reconstructs the same segment quoting and
canonical key requirement (version 4). The static slot selector and
ordinary top-level HoneySQL formatting remain unchanged.

The physical regression exercises reserved names and namespaced keys,
rejects lone noncanonical aliases and simultaneous collisions in supplied
rows, and requires rejection of changed nested values or field identities
without a marker write. The restored canonical observation must commit.
The round-trip check shares XTDB normalization and decoding; it is not
an independent proof of the provider codec, nor a general SQL identifier
formatter qualification. No CLM-R2 premise or certification authority is
retired here.

* v2566 — physical Claim qualification of accumulated guard changes

Requalify the complete physical Claim namespace after v2563–v2565. For
all nine submissions across the four recovery modes, extract the actual
mapped pre-write prefix from the submitted transaction envelope, excluding
the transition-evidence wrapper and the original operation suffix. Check
that prefix against the independent version-4 lowering reconstruction
using the actual actor's source alternative and the corresponding captured
prestate rows. Preserve the existing acceptance/refusal prefix-size and
suffix checks. The independent report retains no certification authority
and does not establish SQL refinement.

The full namespace passed: 3 tests, 811 assertions, no failures or errors.
This includes the two-helper contention race, valid and invalid recovery
constructions, the existing replay, history and publication checks,
pinned snapshot consistency and the refusal-prefix mutation regression.
Production guard semantics are unchanged in this revision. The strict
Chromium gate, Ansatz pilot and full project suite were not run. This is
physical qualification of the fixture, not a universal provider proof or
closure of CLM-R2.

* v2567 — durable mapped guard profile and prefix correspondence

The exact framed preparation now records the current version-4 guard
profile and a canonical structural digest of the ordered formatted SQL
prefix and bound values. The bounded subject encoder tags scalars and
vectors, including UUIDs, before hashing; integer identity is mathematical.
The transition witness advances to profile-version 4 and persists both
fields in the same transaction. At the pinned preceding basis, verification
first checks the abstract prestate and then reconstructs the complete
mapped prefix and compares its identity, including unmapped document
fields. Poststate-only verification checks the profile and digest shape,
not its prestate correspondence. Readback exposes the reconstructed-prefix
check. A digest does not independently authenticate execution or prove SQL
semantics; the trusted admission/handoff and observed basis are premises.

The stronger history audit, chunk and authenticated checkpoint profiles
advance to v4. Older checkpoints cannot skip the new classification
check; the conditional physical-prefix argument requires a v4 report.
Legacy transition witnesses are refused by the current verifier, not
relabelled as if they contained the stronger prefix. This is a deliberate
compatibility boundary; no automatic migration or replay compatibility for
legacy selected mapped evidence is supplied. Full prefix hashing also
inherits the bounded subject encoder's limits. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2568 — durable guard evidence through physical Claim recovery

The complete physical Claim namespace requalifies the version-4 durable
transition witness across the four recovery modes. For each of the nine
actual submitted envelopes, independently reconstruct the exact prefix
from the actor-specific source and captured prestate, then fingerprint
the extracted prefix using the shared canonical digest implementation.
Require the guard profile and fingerprint to agree with both the admitted
candidate and the evidence put, and verify witness command and alternative
identity. Compare the complete durable witness row set with the committed
submissions only. An aborted acceptance cannot leave its witness behind,
even when a successful refusal subsequently uses the same command id.

Check incorporation and replay reports against the persisted profile and
fingerprint and require the reconstructed-preceding-prefix status. Match
verified history transition classifications to the actual committed
(command id, transaction id) pairs, not command identity alone. This is
fixture-level correlation across the observed execution and history path,
not independent authentication of arbitrary writers or proof of the
provider's transaction-internal read basis. Production is unchanged.

The complete namespace passed: 3 tests, 931 assertions, zero failures or
errors. The strict Chromium gate, Ansatz pilot and full project suite were
not executed. No CLM-R2 premise or certification authority is retired.

* v2569 — adversarial durable guard history qualification

Exercise the actual history classifier over four independently initialized
embedded XTDB nodes. Construct a checked framed candidate and its version-4
witness using the issued admission. Deliberately bypass writer mediation
and submit the same guarded domain and command writes with either the
original witness, a fingerprint derived from a different supplemental
prestate field, a legacy version-3 witness, or an obsolete guard profile.
All transactions commit physically. Only the original witness qualifies as
a verified mapped transition; the other three are unclassified with their
specific historical guard/evidence failure reason. Whole and incremental
audits agree, and the conditional observed-prefix argument is withheld.

For each invalid case, authenticate and restore its history checkpoint,
then overwrite the corrupt evidence with the correct witness. The original
unclassified step and cumulative findings persist, and a whole rescan
agrees. Repairing current evidence cannot erase an earlier history failure.
The positive control checks a conditional argument, never a certificate.
This regression passed 1 test / 69 assertions, no failures or errors.
Production is unchanged. This is adversarial fixture-level qualification,
not authentication of unrestricted writers or a provider refinement proof.
The full Claim, Ansatz, Chromium and project suites were not rerun.

* v2570 — historical writer provenance is not guard reconstruction

A correct version-4 witness can accompany raw domain and journal puts
without executing any SQL assertions. Extend the adversarial history
fixture with this unguarded case: the installed adapter refuses the
unmediated envelope before submission, but direct XTDB execution commits
and does not invoke the policy observer. The selected historical rows
still satisfy the expected pre/post correspondence, and reconstruction
of the expected guard digest succeeds. Both guarded and unguarded raw
controls therefore qualify as verified mapped transitions in the
observational classifier. This is not evidence of actual guard execution.

Expose :historical-writer-provenance :not-established in the conditional
physical-prefix premises and retain :historical-mapped-writer-authentication
as an explicit open obligation, independently of unrestricted writer
closure. Neither a clean history nor a correct witness can close it.
Do not alter the history checkpoint profile: the observational classifier
and its persisted state are unchanged. No certificate or production
admission authority is added. Closing provenance would require a trusted
execution receipt bound to the actual committed transaction or an
independently enforced exclusive writer boundary; a digest of a proposed
prefix alone cannot supply either.

* v2571 — retained XTDB source-log transaction observations

The installed XTDB 2.2.0-beta1 internal database interface exposes the
primary source log. Its readRecords range is epoch-qualified and
half-open; an exact transaction-id read can recover a SourceMessage.Tx
whose operations are encoded as an Arrow stream. SQL operation legs
retain statement text and a separate Arrow stream of bound arguments.
The JDBC path rewrites generated question-mark placeholders to numbered
parameters. The opt-in pilot compares the normalized generated prefix
with the retained SQL operations and typed argument rows, in order.
It records non-SQL operation kinds but does not yet decode their payloads.

The physical regression distinguishes a committed guarded submission,
a committed unguarded submission with the same marker write, and an
aborted submission. It recovers the entire generated mapped SQL prefix,
including keywords, strings, integers, booleans, UUIDs and mixed vectors.
The separate xt.txs outcome query is essential: a source log entry alone
records submission, not successful execution. Missing messages and
unsupported epochs provide no correspondence evidence. The internal
in-memory log reads from a retained replay cache; indefinite retention,
local and Kafka storage, remote acquisition, database identity and
trusted source-log provenance require further qualification.

The opt-in pilot passed 1 test / 20 assertions, no failures or errors.
Production history and checkpoint semantics are unchanged. No writer
identity, complete executed envelope, SQL refinement or certificate is
established. Full DML decoding, transaction-outcome correlation and a
trusted retention/acquisition boundary are needed before using this
observation as historical assurance evidence. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2572 — decoded document puts in retained source-log envelopes

Extend the opt-in source-log pilot to decode the ordered put-docs union
legs, including the provider's qualified table tag, complete supported
visible document values, document order and logged internal identifiers.
Check each 16-byte iid against the installed provider's iid function for
its decoded document id. Preserve nested maps, vectors, scalar values,
UUIDs and decoded temporal values; retain the operation-level valid-time
bounds if present. The installed API expands explicit put valid-time
options into individual document rows. The regression compares those
logged bounds as zoned timestamps without discarding their zone.
Unsupported operation legs and unsupported decoded value types refuse
rather than being represented by operation kind alone.

The physical regressions compare the entire supported SQL/put sequence
and distinguish changes to table, document identity, nested value type,
valid-time end, row ordering, journal outcome and operation ordering.
An explicit null field is omitted by the provider's decoded Arrow map;
therefore the observation describes decoded visible entries, not exact
caller bytes, arbitrary SQL normalization or the full physical Arrow
schema. IID verification uses the provider function, not an independent
proof of its encoding. The log's transaction metadata, other operation
families, retention and trusted acquisition remain separate obligations.
The full Claim envelope has not yet been qualified with this decoder.
Production history and checkpoint semantics remain unchanged. No CLM-R2
premise or certification authority is retired.

* v2573 — complete Claim source-log envelope qualification

The first integrated run exposed a provider serialization distinction:
PGWire coalesces adjacent identical SQL statements into one logged SQL
leg with multiple ordered Arrow argument rows, including repeated
parameterless invocations. Decode every row in order, retaining the
original operation spans alongside the expanded logical operation
sequence. The focused regression checks batch multiplicity, order, and
refusal of missing or permuted invocations. The restricted Claim verifier
compares all decoded assertions and document puts, including the journal,
publication intents and durable witness, to the captured submission.
It separately queries the exact xt.txs outcome and refuses missing log
messages, unguarded or changed envelopes, and wrong outcomes.

The opt-in claim mode checks all nine actual Claim submissions in the
complete physical Claim namespace across all four recovery modes. The
five committed submissions use their returned transaction basis; each
of the four aborted submissions is correlated with the unique aborted
ledger step in its fixture, then checked by full envelope equality. This
fixture-specific association is not a general aborted-submission receipt.
The standalone pilot passed 3 tests / 44 assertions; the complete Claim
namespace with log observation enabled passed 3 tests / 989 assertions.
Both exited successfully with no failures or errors.

The verifier is restricted to the fixture's ASSERT and unqualified
canonical put-docs operations; temporal options and unsupported values
refuse rather than being silently erased. The comparison is of decoded
logical operations, not byte-identical caller input or proof of execution
of each assertion. It does not inspect transaction-level options, prove
provider read-basis semantics, authenticate writers, or establish durable
log retention. Production history and checkpoint semantics are unchanged;
ordinary tests do not enable the internal log observer. No CLM-R2 premise
is retired. Ansatz, Chromium and the full project suite were not run.

* v2574 — retained local source-log evidence across node restart

The opt-in source-log pilot now uses persistent local log and storage
paths across two independently opened XTDB nodes in one JVM. Capture
three exact source-log observations and correlate their provider
outcomes: committed guarded and unguarded submissions with identical
visible writes, and an aborted submission with a distinct proposed
write. Close the first node, reopen the same directories, wait for the
last original transaction to be indexed, and require all three decoded
envelopes and outcomes to remain identical. A subsequent committed
update must not substitute its current document for the historical
logged payload or change any original outcome. A future absent source
message remains unavailable.

The initial run exposed a prepared-query-out-of-date conflict on an
immediate schema-dependent query after reopening. Explicitly await the
last original transaction before preparing that query. The corrected
complete pilot passed 4 tests / 69 assertions, zero failures or errors.
The installed LocalLog readRecords implementation reads the retained
file directly. This qualification concerns clean node shutdown and
reopening in one JVM with intact local directories, not process death,
crash recovery, indefinite retention, truncation, Kafka, remote access
or database/log identity authentication. Production history and ordinary
test gates remain unchanged. The full Claim, Ansatz, Chromium and
project suites were not rerun. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2575 — independent JVM source-log restart qualification

Add a separate opt-in process pilot with seed and verify entry points.
Run seed against an existing empty directory, close its persistent local
XTDB node, serialize only decoded observations, exact transaction ids and
process identity to an EDN manifest, and exit. Run verify in a fresh JVM
using the same directories. It reconstructs the expected restricted
operations from its own fixture, checks the original observations and
provider outcomes, and verifies that a later committed update does not
change the original evidence. It also checks the process identity differs
and a missing future record cannot produce an observation.

Reproduction (run the commands sequentially; use a fresh directory):
#+begin_src sh
root=$(mktemp -d)
clojure -M:test -m gesso.assurance.xtdb-source-log-process-pilot seed "$root" &&
clojure -M:test -m gesso.assurance.xtdb-source-log-process-pilot verify "$root"
#+end_src

The two actual JVM invocations passed: seed 1 test / 9 assertions and
verify 1 test / 21 assertions, no failures or errors, both exit status 0.
The execution used the supplied dependency classpath and embedded XTDB
2.2.0-beta1. The manifest is an experimental comparison reference, not
an authenticated receipt or production history checkpoint. The test
requires intact local directories and clean process termination; no
crash durability, retention policy, log identity authentication, Kafka
or provider refinement theorem follows. Production and the ordinary
regression gate are unchanged. The earlier source-log pilot, full Claim,
Ansatz, Chromium and full project suites were not rerun. No CLM-R2 premise
is retired.

* v2576 — bounded embedded XTDB source-log provider interface

Extract the qualified read-only decoder into gesso.assurance.xtdb-source-log.
The opt-in pilot now calls the production namespace and retains its own
restricted expected-envelope construction. The provider returns either a
complete decoded observation with the versioned provider profile, an
unavailable result, or an unsupported result with a typed reason; it does
not return partial operations. Missing records remain unavailable and
unsupported identities, value types and operation legs are refused.
Provider exceptions return a sanitized unavailable result; interruption
is propagated with the interrupt signal restored.

Bound each serialized Arrow message to 16 MiB, each decoded batch to
16384 rows, aggregate charged rows and values to 262144 units, and
recursive value traversal to depth 64. Callers may lower these ceilings
but may not raise them. Limit exact-record acquisition to two observed
records to detect ambiguity. These limits apply after provider record
acquisition and do not sandbox Arrow parsing, bound provider I/O latency
or guarantee peak memory use. The decoded observation contains sensitive
transaction values and is not intended for public reporting.

The initial regression revealed that delete-docs is serialized as SQL
DELETE, not a distinct operation leg. Correct the fixture to check its
actual SQL representation and exercise unsupported values using a float
in a document. The complete pilot passed 5 tests / 83 assertions, zero
failures or errors. Existing retained-envelope, batching and local
node-restart qualification continues through the extracted provider.
The provider remains opt-in and embedded-primary-only; the reported
profile names the tested version, not an independently verified runtime
version attestation. No production history classification, checkpoint,
writer authority or certification semantics change. Independent JVM,
full Claim, Ansatz, Chromium and full project suites were not rerun.
No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2577 — restricted production envelope correspondence

Extract the expected-operation construction and comparison into
gesso.assurance.xtdb-envelope (restricted-envelope-v1). Compare a
nonempty vector of generated ASSERT and canonical public put-docs
operations with the exact retained decoded source message and the
separate xt.txs outcome. Check provider profile and transaction identity
before comparing ordered operations. The report distinguishes matched,
mismatch, unsupported and unavailable; no raw SQL, parameters, documents
or exception payloads are returned. A matched report has no authority
and issues no certificate. The pilot compatibility adapter exercises
this production implementation while retaining independent expected
operation construction for the standalone decoded observations.

Restrict expected SQL to the generated assertion text fragment, rejecting
quoted literals, comments, standalone numbered placeholders and invalid
parameter counts. The initial run exposed unquoted dollar signs inside
normalized journal identifiers; the corrected fragment accepts these
identifier forms. This is not a general SQL parser. Refuse temporal put
options, explicit nulls and unsupported expected operation/value shapes;
bound expected operation count, charged values, text and nesting. These
are decoding/comparison budgets, not a provider resource sandbox.

The corrected complete standalone pilot passed 6 tests / 101 assertions,
zero failures or errors, exit status 0. New cases cover operation and
outcome mismatch, invalid expectations, missing log/outcome, wrong source
identity or profile and sanitized acquisition failure. The initial run
failed with 3 failures and 1 error before the identifier correction.
Full Claim, independent JVM, Ansatz, Chromium and project suites were
not rerun. Historical classification and checkpoints are unchanged;
transaction metadata, expected-envelope provenance, writer identity,
trusted retention/acquisition and provider execution refinement remain
open. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2578 — bind the expected complete restricted transition envelope

The selected mapped publication executor binds the expected ordered SQL
assertions and document puts after adding the framework-owned evidence
absence guard and final evidence put, before handing the vector to the
installed writer. The new :envelope-profile and :envelope-fingerprint
fields in that final witness are the only fields omitted from the digest
input, avoiding self-reference. All other supported operations, typed
values, ordering, journal and publication payloads and witness fields
remain identity-bearing. The digest uses the versioned restricted expected
operation representation and bounded subject encoder, with container and
UUID/temporal tags to avoid encoding aliases. Unsupported operations or
encoder limits can refuse a selected mapped submission before execution.
Transaction-level metadata is not included.

This is an additive extension to the existing version-4 witness, not a
new history classification profile. Historical pre/post checks refuse
malformed binding fields but distinguish a legacy unbound witness
(:not-recorded) from a well-shaped binding (:recorded-unchecked).
The latter is not a historical source-log comparison. Removing both fields
is still indistinguishable from an older v4 witness; mandatory historical
binding and a new history/checkpoint qualification remain separate work.
No writer authentication, transaction-internal read basis, or execution
refinement is inferred from the digest. Physical correspondence reports
expose the unchecked status and fingerprint; the existing abstract
witness equality is retained after excluding only the validated extension.

The mapped projection namespace passed 22 tests / 849 assertions. The
standalone source-log pilot passed 7 tests / 122 assertions, including
mutation of guards, domain writes, command outcomes, publication intents,
operation order, UUID representations and other witness fields. A real
bound envelope matches its source-log record; the unbound expectation
does not. The complete opt-in physical Claim namespace passed 3 tests /
1027 assertions, including all nine actual submitted envelopes and their
bindings and committed readback/replay statuses. All three JVM runs
exited 0 with no failures or errors. Independent JVM restart, Ansatz,
Chromium and full project suites were not rerun. Performance of the new
digest is not separately measured. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2579 — independently recompute recorded transition envelope bindings

Add check-recorded-transition! to the restricted envelope comparator.
The caller supplies the transaction id and an independently read durable
v4 witness, not the original operation vector. Acquire the exact retained
primary source message and reconstruct the restricted caller representation
only if it round-trips exactly through the existing expected-operation
encoder. Check the final logged witness against the supplied witness,
recompute its binding, and separately require a committed xt.txs outcome.
Refuse absent or malformed bindings, nonmatching source identities, altered
operations, placeholder order, IID, bounds, unsupported shapes and aborted
or unavailable outcomes. Reports contain no raw transaction values.

The standalone source-log pilot passed 8 tests / 138 assertions, zero
failures or errors. The first run had one fixture error because execute-tx
throws on an assertion abort; submit-tx and await now retain the aborted
transaction id for checking. Add an opt-in Claim regression comparing
logged operations with independently read durable witnesses; the full
opt-in Claim run exceeded the container execution limit without a final
summary. No Claim pass is claimed. Independent JVM restart, Ansatz,
Chromium and the full project suite were not rerun.

This is a diagnostic comparison, conditional on correct provider decoding,
retention and the independently acquired witness. A self-consistent digest
does not establish that a writer followed the expected semantic plan.
Historical classification and checkpoint profiles remain unchanged. Actual
historical enforcement, original intended-envelope provenance, transaction
metadata, writer authority and provider execution refinement remain open.
No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2580 — require retained envelope comparison in mapped history

Require the v2579 independent comparison after historical mapped prestate
and poststate judgments, using the ledger transaction id and the pinned
transition witness stripped only of system-from. A verified mapped step
now requires :matched with the expected profile, id and fingerprint.
Unavailable, unsupported, mismatched or unexpected results are unclassified;
no source payload is included. An unavailable step is a sticky finding in
that cursor; a fresh audit is needed to re-evaluate the prefix.

Add a narrow adapter operation that resolves a live opaque handle internally
and returns only the envelope check, without exposing its physical node.
Advance whole/chunk history and both in-process and durable checkpoints to
v5. Even correctly authenticated v4 cursors must be rescanned. Operational
physical-prefix arguments require the v5 chunk profile and report recorded
comparison separately from still-open historical writer provenance.

Focused JVM validation: operational report 4 tests / 111 assertions;
selected initialization/history 2 tests / 18 assertions; adversarial mapped
history 1 test / 148 assertions, all green. Initial integration runs exposed
an opaque-handle mismatch and a test double's missing observer arity; both
were corrected. The integration fixture stubs the semantic judgments to
exercise real source-log admission/refusal; the mapped-history regression
uses the actual semantic checks. Missing bindings and removed guards now
fail classification; a deliberately self-bound unguarded raw writer still
passes the conditional comparison. This is an explicit negative control,
not a proof of its authorization. Full Claim, Chromium, independent proof
and full regression gates were not rerun for this revision.

Intended-envelope provenance, source-log decoding/retention refinement,
actual writer authority, transaction-internal reads and infrastructure
writer qualification remain open. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2581 — compare intended mapped guard prefix with retained source

Reconstruct the ordered current-profile mapped prestate guard vector from
admitted mappings and pinned preceding rows, checking the durable prestate
and prefix fingerprint as before. Require this nonempty vector to match the
initial decoded source-log operations exactly, in addition to the recorded
complete-envelope binding, witness equality and committed outcome. Keep
raw SQL and arguments out of operational reports. Preserve the diagnostic
three-argument recorded-envelope API; historical classification requires the
stronger four-argument form and its explicit matched prefix result.

Advance history and checkpoint profiles to v6. Reject previously authenticated
v5 checkpoints; they must be rescanned. Distinguish intended guard-prefix
correspondence in the conditional operational argument. The self-bound
unguarded raw-writer negative control now fails classification even though
its complete envelope digest agrees with its own durable witness.

Focused JVM validation: 8 tests / 311 assertions, zero failures or errors.
This combines the recorded-source comparator regression, mapped adversarial
history, selected history/checkpoint tests and operational report tests.
The comparator rejects absent, empty, non-SQL, wrong-valued and overlong
prefix expectations. Full Claim, Chromium, independent proof and the full
regression suite were not rerun.

This establishes conditional intended guard-prefix correspondence only.
The remaining intended operation suffix, auxiliary and infrastructure writes,
provider decoding/retention, transaction-internal read basis and actual
writer authority are still open. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2582 — compare intended mapped writes with retained source

Select the complete replacement rows for the admitted document write
coordinates from pinned poststate after the existing semantic and input
checks. Check supplemental values, key presence and unchanged fields
against pinned prestate using the admitted document effects. Require all
nonwritten selected rows to be preserved and the journal write target to
be exactly the command slot. This reconstructs a selected-table expectation
from checked historical rows and the admitted source, not from the logged
operations or the caller's original write vector.

Require the retained envelope's complete decoded puts over all mapped
tables and the command journal to equal this expectation with multiplicity.
Grouping of disjoint puts is not asserted. Missing, extra, changed and
repeated selected writes cannot satisfy the comparison. The prior recorded
binding and intended guard-prefix checks remain mandatory. The five-argument
comparator operates through the opaque adapter and returns no raw values.
Advance history, checkpoint and operational prefix profiles to v7; v6
checkpoints require rescanning.

Focused JVM validation: 8 tests / 366 assertions, zero failures or errors.
The adversarial history regression now rejects self-bound transactions
with changed unmapped fields or duplicate mapped puts despite their valid
abstract poststate and matching recorded binding. Selected comparator,
history/checkpoint and operational report regressions passed. The isolated
history fixture still stubs semantic judgments. Full Claim, Chromium,
independent proof and the full regression suite were not rerun.

This is conditional correspondence for complete selected mapped document
replacements and the observed command record, not independently established
command outcome contents beyond the admitted decoder and relation. Extra
assertions, auxiliary/infrastructure writes, transaction metadata, source
log refinement and actual writer/authorization provenance remain open.
No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2583 — verify the complete publication guard and sole evidence write

The actual publication compiler adds an evidence-absence ASSERT before the
admitted mapped guard prefix. The v6/v7 historical checker incorrectly
expected the mapped prefix at operation zero; its synthetic history fixture
omitted this production-owned assertion. Reconstruct the complete prefix
using the pinned command identity and require the leading absence guard
with its exact argument, followed by the independently reconstructed
mapped prefix. Do not change the mapped prefix fingerprint, the writer or
the recorded binding format.

Require exactly one decoded put of the final transition evidence document
across the entire retained envelope, with multiplicity. A self-bound
transaction with an additional intermediate witness write is refused even
if the final pinned witness and selected poststate agree. Report the new
check explicitly in history and operational arguments. Advance history and
checkpoint profiles to v8; v7 and earlier cursors require rescanning.

Focused JVM validation: 8 tests / 437 assertions, no failures or errors.
The mapped-history regression now uses the actual publication prefix and
rejects self-bound missing/wrong evidence guards and duplicate evidence
writes. Also ran the complete opt-in physical Claim JVM source-log suite:
3 tests / 1052 assertions, no failures or errors. This exercises actual

with source-log observation enabled. The full project, Chromium and separate

Remaining assertions and auxiliary/infrastructure puts, transaction metadata,

* v2584 — compare historical publication outbox writes and guards

The selected mapped writer policy admits only the canonical outbox as an
auxiliary table. Extend the historical selected-table expectation to include
all appended outbox rows from the pinned before/after snapshots. Refuse
appended rows when the admission does not declare that auxiliary table.
Always include the outbox table in the envelope comparison, including for
journal-only transitions and other transitions with no observed append.
Require equality of full decoded outbox puts with multiplicity, separately
from mapped and journal puts. Derive the canonical absence assertion for
each expected intent id and require the exact multiset of those assertions
both before the first put and throughout the transaction. Report only the
matched status or a bounded mismatch reason, not intent contents.

Advance historical coverage, authenticated checkpoints and operational
prefix arguments to v9. Previously authenticated v8 cursors must be rescanned.
This checks the observed publication contribution, not the semantic necessity,
cardinality, derived identity, topic, subject or purpose of that contribution
under the admitted source. Other assertion text and unselected tables remain
open; no general assertion equivalence or writer authentication is claimed.

Focused JVM validation: 8 tests / 567 assertions, zero failures or errors.
The adversarial mapped-history regression accepts a guarded append and
refuses self-bound duplicate outbox puts, missing, wrong, late or repeated
canonical absence guards. The zero-append fixture and authenticated v8
checkpoint refusal also pass. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.
The complete opt-in physical Claim source-log suite also passed: 3 tests /
1052 assertions, zero failures or errors. Full project, Chromium and separate
proof gates were not rerun.

* v2585 — check canonical publication records and derived identities

Move the existing frozen v1 UUIDv8 encoding to the outbox namespace so

uses the pinned incorporated command binding and initialized source id,
not an identity claimed by the publisher. For nonempty appended records,
require the full canonical pending document shape, the command's operation,
source and command id, a single qualified purpose and portable nonblank
subjects. Require the set of intent ids to equal the derived slots from
zero through observed cardinality minus one. No row or subject values
appear in mismatch reports. Retained writes and absence guards must still
match the pinned append as before.

Advance history, checkpoints and conditional operational arguments to v10.
Authenticated v9 checkpoints require rescanning. This stronger selected
history profile refuses explicit arbitrary UUIDs even though other
publication APIs can accept them. It does not infer expected cardinality,
slot-to-subject association, topics, purposes or whether publication is
required; an omitted publication or a canonically shaped wrong subject
can still pass this particular check. The shared encoder is not an
independent implementation proof of its algorithm.

Focused JVM validation: 9 tests / 677 assertions, zero failures or errors.
The mapped-history negative controls refuse arbitrary UUIDs, noncontiguous

The full opt-in physical Claim source-log suite also passed: 3 tests /

Add an optional closed publication-derivations catalog to the issued mapped

covering exactly the admitted document write targets, without repeated

Reconstruct expected pending intents independently of the observed outbox
and caller change vector. Given already checked historical document rows,
read old/new subjects in declared order, deduplicate topic/subject changes,
and derive command-bound intent identities over the resulting slots. Refuse

Distinguish a missing declaration from an explicitly empty expectation.

Focused JVM validation: 2 tests / 38 assertions, zero failures or errors.
Tests exercise changed and unchanged subjects, creation, explicit absence,
missing declarations, invalid selectors and source registration drift.
This is an expectation builder, not historical admission. The declaration
is trusted admitted data; its correspondence to installed publication

Full Claim, project, Chromium and separate proof gates were not rerun.

* v2598 — qualify committed stutters from retained operations

Equal selected snapshots alone no longer qualify a committed stutter.

check source profile and transaction identity, and refuse any decoded put,
including a scheduled or unselected write. Require a lossless round trip
through the restricted assertion-only language and a separately observed
committed outcome. Missing or unsupported source operations remain
unclassified. Preserve aborted-stutter and worker classification paths.
Record assertion-only correspondence in the conditional operational
argument. Advance history and checkpoint profiles to v14; v13 and older
checkpoints require rescanning.

findings and valid-time discontinuities. The unavailable-log control is
injected. Physical Claim and mapped-projection report expectations are
updated but those suites, the full project, Chromium and separate proof

Record bounded status only. Advance history, checkpoints and conditional

* v2600 — verify retained outbox worker envelopes

After checking the pinned historical worker state relation, reconstruct
its full ordered SQL guard vector and complete replacement document from

submission. Require the exact retained transaction identity, decoded
operation sequence and independently observed committed outcome. Refuse
omitted, changed or extra guards, extra tables and repeated replacements.
The source decoder now retains SQL timestamp values. A separate restricted
worker comparator accepts only the fixed worker guard forms and compares
SQL timestamps as instants (the provider exposes different UTC zone
representations for SQL and document timestamps). Document values, IIDs,
valid-time bounds and operation order remain exact. The generic mapped
assertion language is unchanged. Report bounded matched status only.
Advance history, checkpoints and operational prefix reporting to v16;
v15 and earlier checkpoints require rescanning.

Chromium and separate proof gates were not rerun.

Preserve the existing restricted ASSERT/put-docs round trip for mapped

only. Independently check the provider's false committed outcome. Expose

JVM initialization/history, manifest and operational-report namespaces:
31 tests / 732 assertions, zero failures or errors. Real embedded XTDB

Qualification establishes a restricted retained attempted operation shape
and recorded abort, not semantic legitimacy, abort cause, rollback semantics,

* v2604 — decompose the provider execution refinement argument

Add a nonauthoritative, portable provider dependency report alongside the
existing conditional physical prefix argument. For its six cases (initial
projection, selected manifest, mapped transition, worker transition,
committed stutter and aborted stutter), report the corresponding checks,
observed counts and open semantic assumptions. Do not infer an observed
case from a vacuously clean prefix. Missing declared residual or publication
expectations mark the mapped case incomplete. An ineligible prefix does
not supply counts or a through basis. Preserve the existing v19 history
and checkpoint formats: this adds no new historical verification.

The common provider premises are exact source decoding and retention,
ledger identity/order/outcome correspondence, pinned system and valid-time
snapshot correspondence, and valid-time continuity. The write cases also
require ordered atomic execution of the complete put sequence, physical
identity and default valid-time semantics, correct ASSERT evaluation, and
the correspondence of transaction-internal reads with the reconstructed
prestate. The worker case additionally requires the transaction clock to
agree with the lease guard and observed transition time. The committed
stutter case requires that supported ASSERTs have no state-changing effects;
the abort case requires that a recorded abort rolls back all attempted
effects, not merely the sampled rows. These are unproved provider premises,
not conclusions drawn from the retained operation comparison. They remain
open even when a case is unobserved, and enter the combined report's open
obligations. Writer authority, application refinement and source-declaration
correspondence remain distinct open obligations.

Final JVM operational-report namespace: 5 tests / 229 assertions, zero
failures or errors. Exercise all six cases, nonempty and empty case counts,
undeclared expectations, source mismatch, stale profile, failed controller,
unclassified history and portable output. Physical Claim integration:
3 tests / 1164 assertions with source-log observation enabled, zero failures
or errors. The installed host report exercises the observed mapped and
worker cases, the aborted case and the unobserved committed-stutter case.
Full project, mapped-projection, Chromium and independent proof gates
were not rerun. No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2605 — introduce independent reference transaction execution

Add a pure selected current-state transaction interpreter independent of
XTDB, its decoder, production constructors and observed transaction outcomes.
Model a canonical table/string-id/document map, ordered whole-document
replacement and literal ASSERT TRUE/FALSE. On false, return the original
state with the failed assertion index. Validate the entire vector before
execution, including the suffix after a false assertion. Unsupported input
has no predicted state or outcome. The first profile intentionally excludes
nested or nullable values, non-string ids, same-batch repeated ids, temporal
options, SQL predicates and all other operations. It is not a temporal or
provider execution model for the full mapped envelope.

Final JVM reference transaction namespace: 3 tests / 107 assertions,
zero failures or errors. A pure unit test and unsupported-input regressions
check the model. An embedded XTDB differential regression starts from a
seed and executes eight successive transactions, carrying forward the
reference's own predictions. Compare complete selected visible row maps
(with duplicate-id checks), independently queried ledger outcomes and
retained envelopes. Exercise whole-document replacement, cross-table
identity, multiple puts, assertion-only commit and rollback after writes.
The initial run passed 89 assertions; an intermediate test-only delimiter
error while adding duplicate-id checks was corrected before the final run.

This is finite empirical evidence for a narrow independent reference
semantics, not a theorem of provider refinement or full mapped transaction
coverage. No production assurance status changes; history remains v19.
Full physical Claim, project, Chromium and separate proof gates not rerun.
No CLM-R2 premise is retired.

* v2606 — model homogeneous parameter equality assertions

Extend the independent reference transaction language to the exact
ASSERT ? = ? operation, with two operands of the same supported domain:
JVM signed 64-bit integers, booleans, bounded well-formed Unicode strings,
restricted simple or qualified keywords, and UUIDs. Equality is exact in
each domain; reject mixed domains, null, floating-point and arbitrary
precision numbers, composite values, malformed Unicode and wrong arity.
The complete vector is still validated before execution, even after a
failing assertion. A false equality rolls back preceding writes and
prevents later writes. Bump the reference profile to v2; history remains
v19.

Final reference JVM namespace: 4 tests / 356 assertions, zero failures
or errors. Eighteen new differential cases compare independently predicted
outcomes, complete selected visible maps and retained envelopes with real
embedded XTDB. Distinct writes on either side of each guard expose a
wrong outcome or incomplete rollback; no provider readback feeds the
next reference prediction. Pure regressions check exact outcomes and
unsupported operands, including an unsupported suffix after a false guard.

This is selected finite empirical evidence, not universal SQL equality,
transaction-internal reads, mapped value coverage or a provider refinement
theorem. No production assurance status changes or CLM-R2 premise is
retired. Full physical Claim, project, Chromium and separate proof gates
were not rerun.

* v2607 — model selected transaction-internal row reads

Extend the independent reference interpreter to the exact quoted-identifier
ASSERT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM table WHERE _id = ? AND field = ?) shape.
The selected fragment uses string row identities and a single flat field
with homogeneous signed long, boolean or well-formed string equality.
The predicate reads the tentative state, not the transaction's original
state or a provider observation. Missing rows or fields cannot satisfy
this equality predicate. Reached mixed-domain comparisons are unsupported;
the entire operation vector is structurally checked before evaluation.
A false predicate restores the original modeled state. Reference profile
v3; history v19 unchanged.

Final reference JVM namespace: 5 tests / 543 assertions, zero failures or
errors. Fourteen additional embedded XTDB differential cases cover reads
before and after replacement, repeated replacements, new rows, absent rows,
removed fields, cross-table isolation, strings and booleans. Continue
comparing complete selected visible maps, retained envelopes and recorded
outcomes while carrying forward only independent reference predictions.
Pure regressions check commit and rollback plus unsupported read inputs.

This is finite empirical evidence for one restricted read shape, not general
SQL, COUNT/NOT EXISTS, mapped predicates, bitemporal semantics or universal
provider refinement. No production assurance status changes and no CLM-R2
premise is retired. Full physical Claim, project, Chromium and separate
proof gates were not rerun.
* v2608 — model selected row and document cardinality assertions

Extend independent reference execution to literal and parameterized
COUNT(*) equality over a whole current table or its string-id selection,
and the exact formatted LENGTH(NEST_ONE (SELECT * FROM table WHERE
_id = ?)) document field-count guard. Counts use the tentative state;
zero row matches establish absence, whereas a missing document cannot
satisfy a positive field count. Count every visible non-null top-level
field, including identity, within the existing flat document fragment.
Restrict SQL identifiers to quoted simple names or unquoted Gesso-prefixed
names, reject invalid counts, arities, identities and unsupported clauses
before execution, and preserve atomic rollback. Reference profile v4;
history v19 unchanged.

Final reference JVM namespace: 6 tests / 768 assertions, zero failures
or errors. Fourteen additional differential transactions cover absent
and populated tables, inserted and replaced rows, distinct identities,
field additions/removals, absent document field counts and rollback.
The harness exercises actual formatted document existence, absence and
field-count assertions, comparing independently predicted outcomes,
complete selected visible maps and retained envelopes. Pure regressions
check unsupported forms and the independently expected sequence results.

This is finite empirical evidence for selected cardinality guards, not
general SQL, nested mapped value predicates, temporal semantics or a
universal provider refinement theorem. No production assurance status
changes or CLM-R2 premise is retired. Full project, physical Claim,
Chromium and independent proof gates were not rerun.
* v2609 — model selected mapped scalar document guards

Extend the independent reference document fragment to restricted keyword
values, including qualified keywords. Require signed JVM longs and
well-formed bounded Unicode strings consistently in stored documents.
Recognize the exact generated mapped scalar COUNT(*) guard with identity
first and three comparison clauses per field: equality, STR equality and
singleton-array STR equality, each binding the identical typed value.
Interpret the combined predicate directly over tentative rows, not by
calling the production formatter or emulating arbitrary SQL STR. Missing
fields fail; homogeneous values compare exactly; keyword/string pairs
are distinct; other reached cross-domain comparisons remain unsupported.
Retain separate field-count guards for additional-field detection.
Reference profile v5; history v19 unchanged.

Final reference JVM namespace: 7 tests / 942 assertions, zero failures
or errors. Thirteen new differential transactions use the actual mapped
scalar formatter and compare outcomes, complete selected visible maps
and retained envelopes, carrying forward independent predictions only.
Pure regressions reject changed repeated operands, invalid guard shapes,
unsupported domains and invalid document values. The selected guard
uses Gesso-prefixed tables and simple unquoted field identifiers; general
SQL identifier validity, nested journal structures, additional scalar
domains and temporal behavior are outside this evidence.

This is finite empirical correspondence, not a universal SQL refinement
or full mapped value language theorem. No production assurance status
changes or CLM-R2 premise is retired. Full project, physical Claim,
Chromium and separate proof gates were not rerun.

Extend independent current-state storage to non-null recursive maps,

value comparison in this revision. Reference profile v6; history v19.

Final reference JVM namespace: 8 tests / 1061 assertions, zero failures
