selected visible maps, outcomes and retained envelopes against embedded
XTDB, including nested writes, repeated replacement, removed fields,
UUIDs, empty collections and rollback. Pure tests reject unsupported
nested shapes, nulls, metadata, noncanonical keys and excessive depth

This is finite empirical nested storage correspondence, not recursive

Recognize the selected occupied journal COUNT assertion with quoted

Recognize whole-vector equality only when followed by every expected

* v2614 — introduce independent bounded bitemporal replay

Add a separate reference temporal interpreter for explicit finite
microsecond-aligned valid-time intervals and strictly increasing supplied
system times. Overlay complete documents on half-open intervals, retaining
unaffected left and right segments and earlier system snapshots. Ordered
puts and literal assertions preserve atomic commit or abort; an abort
retains the prior segments. Reject unsupported operations and malformed
intervals before any replay. The current-state profile remains v9; the
new selected bitemporal profile is v1 and history remains v19.

Temporal JVM: 3 tests / 366 assertions, zero failures or errors. Seven
embedded XTDB transactions with supplied system times compare outcomes
and complete visible row maps at 104 system/valid-time point combinations,
including old snapshots after later corrections, boundary instants,
cross-table and multi-document writes, repeated replacement and rollback.
No observed provider outcome or state feeds reference replay.

This is selected finite point-query correspondence, not complete history
rectangle correspondence, default or unbounded validity, temporal guard
execution, or universal provider refinement. No production assurance
status changes and no CLM-R2 premise is retired. Current-state regression,
full project, physical Claim, Chromium and independent proof gates were
not rerun in this revision.

* v2615 — model default and unbounded valid-time puts

Normalize ordinary table-keyword puts and selected options-map puts to
half-open intervals. With no explicit current-time override, omitted
starts use the supplied transaction system time and omitted ends are
unbounded. Extend interval overlay and point visibility without a
finite infinity sentinel. A bounded correction inside an unbounded
interval preserves the unaffected suffix; an open-ended correction
supersedes scheduled future values. Validate all operations before
replay, rejecting explicit null bounds, invalid intervals and unknown
options. Temporal profile v2; current-state v9 and history v19 unchanged.

Temporal JVM: 6 tests / 838 assertions, zero failures or errors. Nine
additional embedded XTDB transactions compare outcomes and complete
visible row maps at 140 system/valid-time point combinations, including
future schedules, default starts, omitted ends, retrospective correction,
bounded correction of an unbounded interval, cross-table default puts
and rollback. Earlier system snapshots remain independently available.
No provider observation feeds the reference model.

This is selected finite point-query correspondence, not complete history
rectangle correspondence, explicit current-time override semantics,
transaction-internal temporal guards or universal provider refinement.
No production assurance status changes and no CLM-R2 premise is retired.
Current-state, full project, physical Claim, Chromium and independent
proof gates were not rerun.

* v2616 — model bitemporal history rectangles

Extend the independent temporal replay to retain each active segment's
issuing system time. Close the overwritten intersection at the replacing
transaction's system time, retain the origin on unaffected segments, and
suppress zero-duration intermediate versions within a transaction. Abort
retains active and closed rectangles; identical-value writes still have
new provenance. Report the complete selected rectangle inventory with
explicit unbounded ends. Temporal profile v3; current-state v9 and history
v19 unchanged.

Temporal JVM: 8 tests / 869 assertions, zero failures or errors. Three
embedded XTDB histories (21 transactions) compare complete all-valid-time,
all-system-time document inventories and temporal bounds, allowing only
coalescing of adjacent valid intervals with identical document and system
bounds. A new five-transaction history tests identical-value replacement,
overlapping writes within one transaction, rollback and assertion stutters.
Pure regressions check the exact predicted rectangle inventory, and the
previous 244 bitemporal point comparisons remain green. No provider
observation feeds reference replay.

This is finite empirical correspondence for selected put-only histories
and literal assertions, not universal provider refinement, arbitrary
history SQL, deletion, erasure, current-time overrides or temporal guard
execution. No production assurance status changes and no CLM-R2 premise

independent proof gates were not rerun.

Temporal JVM: 11 tests / 1236 assertions, zero failures or errors. Nine

read-your-writes and rollback. Compare outcomes, complete visible maps at

This is finite empirical correspondence for selected default-time guards,

Recognize the exact new-deadline and maximum-duration assertions used

Restrict clock operands to microsecond-aligned Instants in a conservative

and failed assertion indexes. Temporal profile v5; current-state v9

Temporal JVM: 14 tests / 1492 assertions, zero failures or errors. Seven

Compare independently predicted outcomes, complete visible maps at 72
additional bitemporal points and the complete selected rectangle inventory.
Pure regressions reject malformed shapes, unsupported timestamp domains
and altered SQL. No provider readback feeds reference replay.

This establishes selected finite clock-guard correspondence, not worker

Interpret the exact outbox UUID identity and _system_from equality

Admit UUID document identities in the shared reference storage language;

This is selected bounded diagnostic composition, not independent watcher

operation interpretation or universal provider refinement. No production
wiring, certificate or CLM-R2 premise retirement. Full project, physical
Claim, Chromium and independent proof gates were not rerun.

* v2652 — independently interpret selected SQL Arrow operation groups

Add a bounded SQL-only Arrow stream decoder using Apache Arrow FlatBuffer
metadata accessors and direct little-endian data-buffer interpretation,
not the XTDB relation or operation reader. Require the selected schema,
implicit dense-union IDs, one record batch and four-byte zero terminator.
Check buffer extents, nodes, union selection, variable-length offsets,
argument validity and strict query UTF-8. Preserve ordered SQL groups
and opaque nested argument bytes, including absent argument slots.

Focused JVM: 5 tests / 265 assertions, zero failures or errors across
reference Arrow operations and source payload suites. The retained
persistent SQL fixtures match independently specified queries and the
provider decoder before and after reopening. Correct the original test
expectation to reflect XTDB's rewriting of positional placeholders to
$1 and $2. Malformed buffer and framing perturbations reject. XTDB
logged a cancellation during shutdown; the test process exited zero.

Selected SQL-only profile v1. Stock Arrow Java vector loading failed on
the installed implicit union-ID schema during investigation. FlatBuffer
metadata interpretation remains a library dependency, not independently
verified metadata parsing. Nested argument values, mixed operation
kinds, document decoding and full physical operation correspondence
remain open. No production wiring, certificate or CLM-R2 premise
retirement. Full project and independent formal gates were not rerun.

* v2653 — decode selected parameterless Arrow argument relations

Independently qualify the nested zero-column Arrow stream, including
little-endian schema, a single bodyless record batch, positive bounded
row count and exact terminator. An absent argument slot denotes one
invocation; an explicit zero-column relation carries its own count.
Expand ordered SQL groups into empty argument vectors, retaining the
logged operation spans. Reject an entire unsupported stream and bound
total expanded invocations before constructing the result. Preserve
the existing opaque SQL group decoder.

Focused JVM: 6 tests / 330 assertions, zero failures or errors across
reference Arrow operations and source payload suites. The physical
persistent fixture now includes consecutive identical parameterless
statements; independently expanded operations and spans match the
specified expectations and XTDB observations before and after reopening.
Check absent and explicit relations, row boundaries, malformed framing,
multiple batches, trailing bytes and aggregate expansion overflow.
Parameterized retained relations reject without partial operations.

Selected parameterless profile v1. This is decoding of argument row
cardinality, not SQL arity checking or execution semantics. Nonempty
scalar argument relations, mixed operation kinds, independent document
decoding and full provider correspondence remain open. Arrow metadata
accessors remain a dependency. No production wiring, certificate or
CLM-R2 premise retirement. Full project and independent formal gates
were not rerun.

Focused JVM: 7 tests / 405 assertions, zero failures or errors across

* v2712 — characterize equal-system-time assertion visibility

Isolate the v2711 assertion discrepancy using real pinned XTDB SQL
assertions and ordinary puts. A same-time preceding committed row is
visible; fresh tentative rows and replacements of strictly older-system-
time rows are visible to assertions. In the tested overlapping same-time
replacement, the assertion sees the preceding committed value rather than
the tentative value. A guard for the new value aborts with assert-failed;
a guard for the old value commits the replacement. Count remains one.
A subsequent transaction observes the committed replacement. A later
system time restores the tested read-your-write behavior.

Focused JVM: 13 tests / 1727 assertions, zero failures or errors across
reference-temporal-test and reference-equal-time-visibility-test. The new

Do not infer a general tie-breaking rule for all intervals, storage layouts,

* v2713 — characterize equal-time assertion valid-time boundaries

Extend the pinned provider characterization to ten finite half-open
interval arrangements around the assertion's transaction valid-time
point. In the tested single-row cases, a preceding committed row at the
same system time wins if both rows cover that point. A tentative row is
visible when only it covers the point; the prior row remains visible
when only it covers the point, and neither is visible when neither covers
it. Include containment, partial overlap, adjacent exclusive/inclusive
boundaries and disjoint past/future intervals. This is characterization,
not independent replay qualification.

Focused JVM: 14 tests / 2075 assertions, zero failures or errors across

memory and across persistent reopening before attempts and after commits.
Do not generalize to multiple prior segments, multiple tentative writes,

state-dependent replay remains unsupported. No production admission,

and formal gates were not rerun.

* v2714 — characterize repeated tentative equal-time writes

Extend the provider characterization to seven arrangements of multiple
prior temporal segments and two tentative writes, with assertions after
each write. Include older and same-time prior segments, gaps, fresh IDs,
overlapping and disjoint writes. A counterexample prevents generalizing
the v2713 precedence hypothesis: prior same-time writes of value 1 over
[-10,10) and value 2 over [1,10), then tentative value 3 over [-2,2)
and value 4 over [1,5), produce assertion values 3 then 1 at point zero.
The second tentative write does not cover zero, yet changes visibility.
The final committed state at zero is value 3. Preserve characterization
rather than introduce a speculative independent tie-breaking rule.

* v2715 — characterize physical equal-time merge ties

Inspect the pinned xtdb-core 2.2.0-beta1 JAR (SHA-256
e81b66a71af40ebf921cfcacfc52906ca9550734adfd1d34cf7870e5d1a24804).
EventRowPointer comparator orders unsigned IID halves then descending
system-from, with no further tie-breaker. MemoryHashTrie and
MutableMemoryHashTrie compare IID then descending row index within a
leaf. Snapshot.Companion.open appends committed live table and open
transaction table snapshots separately; scan.clj adds persisted segments
and then live snapshots to merge planning. ScanCursor.tryAdvance polls
one leaf pointer from a priority queue, calculates its polygon, emits
nonzero intersecting put rectangles, advances that pointer and reinserts
it when valid. PolygonCalculator.calculate updates a temporal ceiling
in encounter order. The tied cross-leaf merge therefore does not define
an independent logical latest-write precedence.

Exercise actual pinned EventRowPointer, PriorityQueue and PolygonCalculator
with synthetic leaf relations in the v2714 counterexample's order. Prior
leaf [2,1] and tentative leaf [3] yield scan order [2,3,1] and visible
value 3; tentative leaf [4,3] yields [2,4,1,3] and visible value 1.
Reversing leaf insertion yields [4,2,3,1] and visible value 3. This
reproduces the counterexample at the physical primitive level and shows
leaf-order sensitivity, not an instrumented trace of the full provider
scan or a guarantee that actual storage reorganizations change results.

Focused JVM: 16 tests / 2619 assertions, zero failures or errors across
reference-equal-time-merge-test, reference-equal-time-visibility-test
(4/971 combined), and reference-temporal-test (12/1648). New physical

change; equal-time state-dependent replay remains unsupported. Prefer a
strict-clock assurance boundary over modeling unspecified merge ties;

* v2790 — explicit durable source and historical lineage obligations

Add a narrow descriptive constructor using the existing unadmitted
assumption vocabulary, with three separate named obligations scoped
to one comparison, its publication source, original observation
reference, retained acquisition reference, relation, versioned identity
policy name and named trusted boundaries. The two acquisition premises
require actual binding of the original execution observation and the
complete independently acquired retained sample to their respective
storage incarnations. The third premise depends on both and requires
the selected identity relation at the observed cut.

Distinguish two relations:
- Same store incarnation: continuity of one operationally identified
  store across lifecycle events, not equality of paths, stored names,
  content hashes or individual filesystem inodes.
- Authorized historical lineage: policy-authorized descent preserving
  the required historical prefix, including legitimate restoration
  and replica acquisition. It need not preserve the physical incarnation.
  It does not establish an exclusive canonical writer, promotion
  authorization, absence of forks or permission for future writes.

For an actual identity claim, a trusted original-source observation
and a trusted complete acquisition must bind the exact original and
target incarnations and cuts to the comparison. The identity authority
must independently justify the specified relation under its named
policy. Operational continuity may instead remain an explicit trusted
environment assumption, propagated into any conditional result. A
copied history, stored UUID, equal digest or self-asserted identity
cannot prove either relation by itself. An authorized restore may
satisfy lineage but not incarnation continuity. Matching historical
content must not establish identity or current authority.

The new API describes obligations only: references and boundary
names are declarations, not issued identities, trusted observation
handles or proof terms. It has no provider access, evidence admission,
lineage checker or discharge path. Both identity claims remain
:not-established, all premises :open, authority :none and certificate
:not-issued. Existing recovery diagnostics and runtime admission
are unchanged.

* v2795 — durable identity trust boundary and lifecycle policy

** Decision and scope

Use an explicit trusted deployment/lifecycle premise for the initial
single-node profile. Do not require a new identity service, cryptographic
journal or remote coordination system for ordinary Gesso applications.
A deployment may later supply stronger independently qualified evidence;
that is a separate identity-policy profile, not a change to operation
semantics or a prerequisite to running an uncertified application.

The logical publication source identifies the application source domain.
A storage incarnation identifies one operationally controlled store.
A host/session identifies a particular running instance of that store.
None of these identifiers is a credential. A durable incarnation claim
requires an independent lifecycle binding, not just an equal source ID.
The locally issued handles from v2791–v2794 remain process-local; they
cannot be serialized, restored or promoted into durable credentials.

** Required trust boundary

The selected policy must identify the deployment/controller responsible
for opening and closing the actual embedded store, and for authorizing
restoration or replica creation. A stronger evidence profile must obtain
its lifecycle records from an authority outside the copyable store and
bind them to the actual opened provider (log and storage together). The
authority and its evidence acquisition must themselves be qualified.
Reading a sidecar UUID, directory name, inode, config, digest, manifest
or signed but unbound historical record from the store cannot do this.
A trusted controller that does not control out-of-band substitution must
name that limitation as an environment assumption. There is no claim of
resistance to an administrator who can replace both store and authority.

The original observation binding must be made while the originating
provider and issued observation are available. Preserve the comparison
scope, source domain, selected identity policy, original incarnation,
observation reference and exact relevant historical cut. The acquisition
binding must independently join the complete selected retained sample to
its actual target incarnation and cut. The relation authority must use
these bound endpoints, not endpoint labels copied from supplied reports.
If origin evidence was never captured, do not reconstruct it by matching
later content. Any retrospective continuity claim remains an assumption.

The cut is source-relative: the required ordered prefix and transaction
identities, including aborted entries where relevant, and the qualified
processed frontier. Numeric transaction IDs or timestamps alone are not
portable across unrelated stores. Preserve exact selected-content and
physical correspondence checks in addition to the identity relation.
Even a legitimately identified store may have been rolled back or lost
history; identity alone cannot establish prefix preservation.

** Lifecycle rules for the selected relations

| Event | Same incarnation | Authorized historical lineage |
|-------+------------------+-------------------------------|
| Managed close/reopen of the original store | Conditional on trusted uninterrupted custody | Conditional on authorized custody and preservation of required prefix |
| Arbitrary byte copy or unrecorded replacement | Not established | Not established |
| Explicitly authorized restore to a new store | No | Conditional on authorization and required prefix |
| Explicitly authorized replica acquisition | No | Conditional on authorization and required prefix |
| Rollback below the required cut | Does not alone settle identity | Refuse the selected historical claim |
| Unknown lifecycle or missing original binding | Not established | Not established |

For a restore, a new incarnation must be distinguished from its parent;
restoring over the old path does not make it the same incarnation. The
lineage claim is directional, scoped to the required historical cut.
Multiple authorized descendants may exist. A chain of authorized descent
can support lineage only if each edge is qualified and preserves the
required prefix. Neither relation authorizes writer promotion, current
policy, exclusive leadership, nor continued absence of forks.

** Admission and acceptance plan

Keep =reference-storage-identity/describe-obligations= as the sole
source of the three scoped open obligation descriptions. A selected
operational-premise path must preserve all three exact premise identities
and their dependency closure; accepting trust is not proof discharge.
Reports must explicitly distinguish conditional historical correspondence
from an independently established identity relation, carry the named
trusted assumptions, and never emit a positive identity or certificate
from an arbitrary caller-supplied record or Boolean. Missing, changed or
unaccepted mandatory premises block the stronger claim, not the existing
content-only diagnostic. Do not weaken the original local comparison.

Next implement a narrow conditional-result composition using the existing
assumption vocabulary: version the comparison scope to bind the selected
cut explicitly (the v2790 reference strings alone do not specify it),
derive the required premises and require exact structural correspondence
of explicitly selected premises, without admitting or discharging them.
Exercise missing,
substituted, wrong-relation, wrong-cut and policy-mismatched premises and
verify that no unconditional authority follows. Separately qualify the
actual deployment bindings if a stronger durable identity claim is needed.
The future multi-node deployment must supply its own qualified lifecycle
and source-domain policy; the single-node operational premise cannot be
silently extended to it.

Documentation-only decision. No runtime change, new trust grant, identity
establishment, authenticated writer provenance, certificate or CLM-R2
premise discharge. No tests run for this revision.

Version the storage identity obligation scope to v2. Require an explicit

Validate bounded vectors, strictly increasing nonnegative offsets, origin
at offset zero for nonempty history, ordered transaction membership and
frontier equality with the final record (or -1 for empty history). The
entire declaration participates in all three premise identities. Refuse
v1 scopes that omit the cut rather than silently migrating their premises.

The required cut describes a prefix; the target acquisition may extend
it. No prefix extraction, actual sample binding, transaction outcome
verification, lifecycle authority or premise selection is introduced.
The digest and resulting declarations are sensitive internal comparison
material, not public receipts, and shape validation is not evidence.

The source-level argument is by structural induction on the admitted

the same scalar from the checked binding or present field observation.

Negation and implication preserve truth by the induction hypothesis.
For the And/Or loops use the number of remaining operands as the decreasing
measure: the empty suffix returns true/false respectively; a decisive
child returns false/true, otherwise the next suffix is evaluated. Since
all operands of this restricted fragment are total Boolean expressions
on admitted valuations, short-circuiting preserves abstract truth.
